Roundtrip + give-back carry the whole meter; stops is weak supporting context; ATR is noise — exactly as the design note claimed. The 40/40/20 blend is right to lean on the two that work. The 65 cutoff wants no change — it caught the −$905 with room to spare and produced zero false STAY-OUTs on green days. One refinement worth a look: both CAUTION days (47, 56) still lost money, so CAUTION isn't costless — consider making CAUTION an action (faders-off / veto-gates-only / half-size) rather than just a label. n=2, so propose-and-validate, don't hard-wire.

6 · Bench-decision review — grading the week's actual benches

The Friday US-open sequence is the teaching case. Operator said "enable all gates, US OPEN" and left; the open printed a real up-thrust (ER 0.68, break 28713) that then fully reversed (−172pt roundtrip). Here is exactly how the six gates behaved, live:

Gate armed at the opentypeopen P&Llesson
abs_veto (55s-veto protected)VETO-gate+$146caught the thrust, banked both legs, held disciplined
grind (no veto)CHURNER−$370bought the rollover top, 4 stops in 90s
exhaustion_short (off-regime)FADER−$72fired in ATR-62 violence — a 0.5R scalp can't cover a 62pt stop
Banked lesson [[us-open-arm-veto-gates-not-churners]]: at a violent open, arm the veto-protected gates (abs_veto) and keep the churners (grind) and off-regime faders benched. Enable-all guarantees the wrong-side gates churn the reversal before anyone can bench them. The defensive trim that followed (grind→capit/rgv→exhaustion→fully-flat) was shadow-vindicated: every benched gate's shadow bled through the ATR-52/62 chop.

The individual bench grades:

  • grind benches — right when the trend structure breaks (07-31 open reversal, 07-30 14:51 ER-collapse: both GOOD); wrong when the trend is intact and it's just open-noise (07-30 13:36: BAD). The distinction is roundtrip + ER-collapse, and the meter now measures exactly that.
  • abs_veto scalp-then-bench — the fire-and-stop exception to its veto protection worked (07-30 22:10 bench after it stopped both legs = GOOD). But re-enabling it into a choppy recovery (07-31 pm, ×2, −$245) was CHURN: the 55s veto is a microstructure filter, so a chop micro-thrust passes it. Rule holds: don't re-arm even abs_veto until a genuinely clean trend (ER≥0.35 sustained).
  • the exhaustion_short live trial — deployed the 0.5R/1.5R fade-scalp; it fired once, off-regime, in the open violence (net −$72, as predicted for high ATR). The core thesis (fader-scalp in normal chop, ATR 11–18) is still untested — one off-regime fire is not a verdict. Keep it pinned and running into more low/mid-ATR roll-over afternoons before judging.
  • the fully-flat "stay-out" calls — the best calls of Friday. Once the day was read untradeable, going flat banked zero further bleed; the meter held 74–96 STAY-OUT the whole time confirming it. Stay-out is a real, earned decision, not a cop-out.

7 · Durable router & architecture — is polling the right mechanism?

Did the durable 5-min systemd tick manage correctly? Yes. It is active / enabled, fires on schedule, and across its two live days it made 2 autonomous switch-changes (07-30 22:10 bench abs_veto after fire+stop, 22:15 bench the 3 shorts + grind under a fresh UP bias — both graded GOOD) and then correctly logged "changed: none" through the entire Friday untradeable day. No thrash, no naked positions, no double-writes, zero real leakage. The robustness fix works: the desk was never unmanaged.

Is polling the best mechanism, though? Here is the honest cost of the 5-min poll. The router only acts on the tick boundary, so when a regime flips mid-window, up to 5 minutes of churn can print before the switch changes. The clearest single instance this week: on the Friday open, grind bought the reversal and lost −$370 over ~4 minutes before it was benched. A per-gate in-code regime-guard (an entry-time veto that blocks a counter-regime fill in milliseconds, no polling) would have stopped most of that at the source.

Mechanismreaction lagaddressable leak this weektrade-off
5-min poll (current durable)up to 5 minbaselinesimple, fail-safe, survives session exit; laggy on fast flips
event-watcher (in-chat, current)~secondspartialalready cuts lag on breaks/bleed — but dies with the session
in-code per-gate regime-guard (proposed)<1 sec, at entryup to −$956 / −$1,933catches the counter-trend NEAR-MISS bucket the poll+coarse-threshold lets through

Where that leak number comes from (regime-segmented, per the discipline): the study's trade classification buckets the fader trades by regime-context. The "counter-trend near-miss" bucket — trades that entered against a detectable regime and lost — is n=15 / −$956 (live set) and n=32 / −$1,933 (full set). That is the leak a millisecond in-code guard could address that a 5-min poll structurally cannot. Recommendation: keep the poll as the fail-safe floor, but scope an in-code counter-regime entry-veto on the momentum gates as the next build — it attacks the exact segment (counter-trend fills in a live trend) where polling loses. Flagged as a build, not a Saturday switch.

8 · Verdict & Saturday recommendations

Split verdict — and the two halves point opposite ways. Is the router earning its keep? Yes — as a defense. The defence earns its keep on exactly one cut, and it is the one that survives everything: benching the FADERS is +$810 (n=25, non-overlap) and comes out identical under every independent regime tag anyone could throw at it. Benching into CHOP is not in that class — it scores +$612 under the router's own tag, +$187 under ADX(14) and −$382 under a 60-minute regression tag, i.e. it only holds if you accept the router's own definition of chop. And the offence does not earn its keep: the "stop benching momentum in TREND_UP, +$1,615/week" finding was independently re-derived this weekend and is WITHDRAWN (Part 3.5). Zero real blocked-loss leakage on the days it ran durably, +$807 of shadow-confirmed defence on 07-30, and a durable tick that held flat through the worst day of the week without a session driving it — those all stand. The desk finished +$625 as booked, −$598 with your hands off (see Part 1) through a week that contained a −$905 day. ▶ And the finding that outranks this entire section: as of 08-01 all six gates are PINNED in router_tick_durable.py, so the apply step's change set is permanently empty and the durable tick has benched nothing for 36 hours. The proven half of the machine is switched off. That is MONDAY play #1. The weakness is offensive: it over-benches aligned momentum in trends (opportunity-cost, not bleed) and the exit-selector left $5.6k of regret on the table (separate machine, separate review).

But this is one week, one summer range regime, in-sample, thin realised n, partly already gated. The regime→exit R-target cheat-sheet (faders 0.5/1.5, momentum 1.5/2.5, trend 2.5/wide) remains an unproven operator prior — the exhaustion 0.5R/1.5R trial has fired exactly once, off-regime, so those Rs are NOT yet proven and should be swept per-regime once a trend week supplies the n. Part 3 The Rev2 & Rev3 corrections — the new material, in full

Everything in this part was written after the report went out. Parts 3.1–3.4 are the five Rev2 answers that never landed in the published file. Parts 3.5–3.7 are three independent weekend re-derivations that were commissioned to break those answers — two of them succeeded, and where a Rev3 section disagrees with a Rev2 section, the Rev3 section is the correction. Part 3.8 is the audit that caught the failed fold-in in the first place, printed in full because it is the receipt for this revision.

Rev 2 · Q1 What the desk made without your hands on it

PATCH MARKER — this section patches and extends PART 1 (“The Live Desk”): it restates Part 1’s headline number, its six-gate card table, its exit-mix table and its “the tail is the edge” claim on automated fills only (MANUAL_CLAIM excluded), and it re-runs Part 1’s SURVIVOR — steadiest verdict on exhaustion_short against that same automated-only tape. It also extends Part 2.6 (Router review) with the regime split of the automated book. Everything below is computed from data/gazbot7.db (venue truth) and data/capture.db 250 ms ticks. Where Part 1 and this section disagree, this section is the correction.

Garrath — you asked the right question, and the answer is not comfortable. Hands off, the desk lost $598 this week. The +$625 headline in Part 1 is real money on a real ledger, but $1,223 of it came from twelve trades you closed by hand with the Claim-profit button. Strip those twelve and what the machine did on its own is 194 fills, −$598, 35.1% win, −$3.08 a trade, profit factor 0.91. Part 1 led with a survivor; on automated fills that survivor is not green. But it is not the disaster it first looks either — and the reason it broke is a config change you made on Wednesday evening, not a regime. Below: the honest split, the tick-by-tick counterfactual of what the robot would have done with those twelve trades if you had sat on your hands, the re-run survivor verdict, and the re-nomination.

The one-paragraph answer

Automated-only: −$598 over 194 fills (35.1% win, PF 0.91). The twelve hand-claims were +$1,223 at 12-for-12. Repriced on 250 ms ticks under the exit config that was actually loaded at the time, those same twelve entries — the entries were automated; only the exits were yours — would have banked +$360.5, five of the twelve stopping out. So a fully hands-off week is −$237.5, not +$625, and your hands were worth +$862.5. The nominated survivor, exhaustion_short, is −$56.0 on 44 automated fills — flat, not green. Its “+$372.5 four-in-a-row” on Thursday was four hand-claims, and three of those four were sitting on positions the tape later ran 49 to 98 points through the stop. SURVIVOR — steadiest does not survive as a money claim. It does survive as a mechanism claim, but only on the exit it was running until Wednesday 19:10 UTC.

Caveat, stated plainly: two of the five counterfactual stops (#424, #429) are sub-one-point grazes — 0.57 and 0.82 pt through — and a resting stop that thin is genuinely a coin-flip on fill. And note the direction of the remaining error — the reprice fills at the exact stop level with no slippage, and our own live-vs-model work says real stops fill 1.1–3.1× worse. So +$862.5 is a floor on what your hands were worth, not a ceiling.

Verdict: the money claim fails, the mechanism claim survives — conditionally. As a gate, exhaustion_short made −$56 automated. It is still the steadiest thing on the desk by every dispersion measure — 50% win, a median trade of essentially zero (+$0.8), PF 0.96, green on 3 of 5 sessions, and the smallest leave-one-day-out damage of any gate that traded in size (−$151, against −$618 for abs_veto_long and −$731 for grind_long) — but “steadiest” now means flattest, not best earner. Part 1’s offensive evidence for it, the +$372.5 four-in-a-row on Thursday afternoon, was four hand-claims, zero automated fills, and under the exit config actually loaded that sequence would have been −$51.2. That is the single most load-bearing correction in this section.

What actually killed it was a config change, not the tape. The pre-Wednesday configuration was non-negative in all three regime buckets it traded — clean-trend +$436.5 (11 fills, PF 3.66), in-between-building −$84.5 (13, 61.5% win), violent-whipsaw +$13.0 (13, PF 1.04). That is genuine regime robustness, on small n. The scale-out configuration then lost −$321.5 on four fills in violent-whipsaw alone, at 0% win. A fader was put on a 2.5R target and a wide trend-riding trail; faders do not get 2.5R in a whipsaw. Your hands on Thursday were, in effect, manually re-implementing the tight exit the slate had just removed — and your Friday 17:29 exit_overrides.json (exhaustion 0.5 / 1.5) was you noticing the same thing and automating it.

Honesty on concentration: the +$365 pre-Wednesday slice is not evenly earned — its best single trade is 28% of gross profit and stripping its top three leaves −$285 over 34 fills. It is a real but thin edge, and it is in-sample.

That fourth row is the important one and it cuts against the tight-exit story: in violent whipsaw, tightening does not save the fader — the k1.5 chandelier is the single worst config in that bucket (−$202). This is the bucket the gate took 28 of its 62 entries in. The lever is not the exit, it is not being armed. That matches the standing ledger lesson (07-31: violent post-thrust chop → go fully flat) and it is the recommendation.

The momentum prior fares worse. Sweeping abs_veto_long’s 42 automated entries the same way: the plateau is at 0.75–1.0R (+$292 / +$532, 54.8% win) and it collapses at exactly the numbers you guessed — 1.5R +$26, 2.0R −$42, 2.5R +$75 — with the lock-chandelier at −$16 overall and −$415 in clean-trend. The 1.5 / 2.5 momentum prior is falsified for abs_veto_long on this tape; it is holding a thrust far too long. For abs_veto_short the prior survives: the plateau is 1.25–2.0R (+$178 / +$202 / +$188) and 1.5R is the only config in that sweep with a positive leave-one-day-out floor (+$45.4, 3 of 4 green days). Same family, opposite sides, opposite answers — which is itself the argument for per-side sweeps over roster-wide priors.

Three things to actually do

  1. Put exhaustion_short back on a tight exit and stop feeding it whipsaw. The Friday override (0.5 / 1.5) was the right instinct at the wrong numbers — the sweep says Lot A 0.75R, Lot B k1.5 chandelier, and it says the exit cannot rescue a violent-whipsaw entry at all, so the router bench matters more than the R. This is the single highest-value change in this section: it is worth roughly +$420 on the twelve claimed trades alone, and it converts the hand-claim habit into a mechanical rule.
  2. Re-sweep abs_veto_long’s exit before next week. Its loaded 1.5/2.5 config sits on the falsified side of a clear plateau; 0.75–1.0R is +$532 against +$26 on this tape, on 42 entries. This is the cheapest red-to-green candidate on the desk.
  3. Instrument the claim button. Twelve hand-claims at 12-for-12 is not luck, it is a signal the machine is missing — and right now it is invisible in every P&L we report. Every MANUAL_CLAIM should be auto-repriced against what its slot’s exit would have done and logged as a delta, so “Garrath’s hands are worth $X/week and here is the pattern they exploit” becomes a standing number instead of a Friday archaeology dig.
Method & limits — read before quoting any number above

Venue truth vs model. Every figure in fact tables 1, 2, 4 and 5 is straight from data/gazbot7.db trades — real fills, fees included, nothing modelled. Fact tables 3 and 6 are reprices on 250 ms ticks from data/capture.db with $1.50 round-turn fees, fills assumed at the exact stop/target level and no slippage. Our own execution work says live stops fill 1.1–3.1× worse than modelled, so every reprice number is a ceiling; use them to rank configs against each other, never as a P&L promise.

Sample size. 194 automated fills over six sessions. Regime buckets: violent-whipsaw 81, clean-trend 64, in-between 40, normal-chop 9, dead-chop 0. The sweeps use 64 (exhaustion), 42 (abs_veto_long) and 22 (abs_veto_short) entry events over nine, six and four days. Anything below n≈15 in this section is flagged inline and should be treated as a direction, not a measurement.

Known modelling weakness. 17 of the 64 exhaustion replays never hit a stop, target or trail inside the 120-minute / session-flat cap and are marked at the last tick — the same 17 entries in every config, so they do not tilt one config against another, but they do mean roughly a quarter of the sweep is marked-to-market rather than exited. Separately, an entry that hits its target before the tape later runs through its stop is scored as a win (correctly — the order of events is respected), which is why trade #444 in fact table 3 shows a target despite the tape eventually running 85 pt against it.

Not swept, and it should be. grind_long’s 2.5 / wide pair was not put through this ladder — it is the largest automated loser on the desk (−$421.5) and it has the two graze-sensitive counterfactuals in fact table 3. That sweep is the obvious next job and its absence is a hole in this section, not a silence.

Reconciliation. Week bounds and totals match Part 1 exactly (206 fills, +$625.0); the day-by-day rows here use the UTC close-date rather than Part 1’s Paris session boundary, which moves trades between days but not the week total or any gate total.

REV2 · Q2 Is abs_veto_long already live — and if it is, what exactly am I promoting?

Garrath — you are right and Part 2 is wrong. abs_veto is live on both sides and has been since 2026-07-25 (slot_strategy.tournament_slots(), commit eb57f64); abs_veto_long is armed in gate_switches.env right now and traded 59 lots across 43 signals for +$59.5 this week. There is no slot to promote. What is actually on the table is a four-line config diff on a gate that is already running — and the diff is not the one Part 2 proposed. Chasing your +$59.5-vs-+$776 question turned up three things: (1) the two numbers are measured over different windows, so the real gap is $609.5, not $717; (2) 82% of that gap is entry, not exit — the live desk only ever saw 43 of the shadow's 69 long signals, because the router had the gate benched for 26 of them; and (3) while checking why the ER floor did not block anything, I found that the dual-slot scale-out slate silently killed every ER and ATR floor on the desk on 2026-07-29. Both this report and the router have spent the week reasoning about filters that are not running.

Honesty banner. Every number below is from the live gazbot7.db trade ledger, the tick-repriced shadow_real.real_pnl, the gazbot7-tournament journal, and 10.08M captured MNQ ticks — nothing is recalled. Windows are stated on every row. The tape is one summer week, chop-dominated, MNQ-only, PAPER: the CLEAN-TREND bucket is n=6 long / n=5 short and is reported but not scored. The shadow runs 1 lot, the live desk runs 2 — no raw dollar comparison between them is valid without that adjustment, and I make it explicit everywhere below.

1 · The premise — both numbers are real, but they are not the same measurement

The +$776 is the shadow's Mon-to-Fri calendar week. The +$59.5 is the live gate's whole life this week, which starts at the Sunday 22:00 UTC reopen. The shadow lost $106.5 on that Sunday night; crediting it with a session the live desk actually traded inflates the gap by $107.

windowenginesignalslotsnetnote
Mon 07-27 → Fri 07-31shadow abs_veto_55s LONG6666+$775.5the "+$776" in the question
Mon 07-27 → Fri 07-31LIVE abs_veto_long4056+$131.0the like-for-like live number for that window
Sun 07-26 22:00 → Fri 07-31shadow abs_veto_55s LONG6969+$669.0the honest comparison window (live gate's first fire → last)
Sun 07-26 22:00 → Fri 07-31LIVE abs_veto_long4359+$59.5the "+$59.5" in the question
Sun 07-26 22:00 → Fri 07-31shadow abs_veto_55s SHORT6363+$659.0the short side, same window
Sun 07-26 22:00 → Fri 07-31LIVE abs_veto_short2226+$358.5live SHORT beat shadow per signal: +$16.3 vs +$10.5

And the trade counts are not near-identical. 59 live "trades" are 59 lots — under the dual-slot scale-out every signal opens two sub-slots (_A and _B). Collapse them and the live desk took 43 signals against the shadow's 69: it saw 62% of the opportunity set, at 2× the size on each one it did take. That single line reframes the whole question — it is not "the same trades earned less", it is "a different, smaller, double-sized trade set".

2 · The config diff — live abs_veto_long/_short vs shadow abs_veto_55s

The entry is byte-identical. Everything that differs is arming, sizing, exit and execution.

The live veto is doing almost all its work on the short side. From the tournament journal, 07-26 → 07-31: abs_veto_long raised 63 thrusts and opened 59, skipped 4 as stale, vetoed 0 as absorbed. abs_veto_short raised 80 and opened 30, skipped 28 as stale, vetoed 22 as absorbed. So the "absorption veto" is a short-side filter in practice; on the long side it is a 55s delay and nothing more. That is worth knowing before anyone calls the veto the reason abs_veto works.

3 · ★ The thing I did not go looking for: every ER and ATR floor on the desk has been dead since 07-29

tournament.step() gates each intent with er_blocks(slot, er) and atr_blocks(slot, f.atr) — passing the slot tag. Under the dual-slot scale-out slate the tags are abs_veto_long_A, abs_veto_long_B, grind_long_A… but ER_FLOOR and ATR_FLOOR are keyed on the base names. The lookup never matches. The same function has a _base() helper and it is applied to the bench switch (line 250), the 55s veto (line 303) and the exhaustion confirm (line 345) — but not to the two floors.

evidencewhat it shows
codetournament.py:139/142 — er_blocks(slot, er) / atr_blocks(slot, f.atr) with the sub-slot tag; deciders.ER_FLOOR / ATR_FLOOR keyed on base names.
journal7,734 ER gate: / ATR gate: suppression lines between 07-26 and the very last one at 2026-07-29 13:29:04. The scale-out sub-slots first appear in the journal at 2026-07-29 16:24. Zero suppressions since.
tradesabs_veto_long_A opened at 30-min ER 0.013 (07-30 10:09, +$77.5 TARGET), 0.035 (07-30 05:48), 0.049 (07-30 12:32) — all far below the 0.25 floor that is supposedly gating it.

Which floors are affected: abs_veto_long ER 0.25, grind_long ER 0.35 and ATR 24, abs_veto_short ATR 16, capitulation_long ATR 10. All of them, since 07-29 16:24. Note the date on the abs_veto floor: commit 9767c67, 2026-07-30 — it was written the day after the slate change disabled it, so it has never once run live.

This corrupts two conclusions elsewhere in this report. Part 2.6 quotes the router's own 07-30 11:08 tick — "grind re-armed but its 0.35 floor auto-gates it, so low-risk" — and the 11:13 post-mortem "fired at ER≥0.35 (floor passed)". Neither is true; grind had no floor, and the 11:13 loss is not evidence about the floor's quality. Any Part 1.5 / Part 2.6 line that reasons from a live ER or ATR floor after 07-29 16:24 needs re-reading with the filter removed.

Do NOT rush to wire it up. The counterfactual on abs_veto_long's 31 scale-out-era signals is honest but tail-dominated:

populationsignalsnetmedianstrip best-1strip best-2
would have been BLOCKED by a working ER≥0.2524+$494.5−$42.8+$18.0−$233.5
would have been KEPT (ER ≥ 0.25)7−$168.5———

Read it straight: the floor would have blocked the week's two biggest winners (+$476.5 and +$251.5) and kept a −$168.5 book — but the blocked population's median is −$42.8 and its edge vanishes after stripping one trade. So there is no live evidence for the floor and none robustly against it. It is simply untested, and the honest position is: fix the wiring so the desk stops lying about itself, then decide the floor's value on its own evidence — do not let a silent bug make the decision.

4 · The reconciliation — same signals? same fills? where did the $609.5 go?

Method: match every shadow abs_veto_55s fire to a live signal within ±180s. Anything left over is a live gate that never raised the signal at all.

The shape is consistent across both engines and both sides: abs_veto is an OVERNIGHT NORMAL-CHOP gate. 6 · The 1.5/2.5 exit cells — swept, not assumed

The Lot-A/Lot-B Rs in exit_overrides.json are an operator prior, so I swept them tick-honest. Method: 150 abs_veto_55s entries inside the 250ms tick coverage (10,080,959 MNQ ticks, 2026-07-23 22:00 → 07-31 21:00), each replayed forward from the fill — a lot banks +R×ATR if the favourable level prints first, stops at −1.0×ATR if the adverse level prints first, else marks out at the 90-min cap (no shadow trade reached it; max hold was 62 min). Replay validated against the shadow repricer: 98.7% sign agreement, MAE $2.70 per trade at the shadow's own 2.0R setting — so the sweep is measuring the exit, not my simulator.

Verdict on the guess. On the LONG side the live cell A1.5/B2.5 is the worst of the eleven cells swept, on every measure: +$13.6/signal vs +$22.7 for A1.0/B1.5, LODO-min +$4.7 vs +$16.8, strip-best-3 +$6.8 vs +$19.0. The plateau is broad and sits at A≈1.0, B 1.5–2.0 — a genuine parameter plateau, not a spike (0.75/1.5, 1.0/1.5 and 1.0/2.0 are all within $4 of each other). On the SHORT side every cell lands between +$15 and +$18 per signal and the LODO/strip ordering flips between them — the R choice is noise on the short side, so leave it alone. What is not noise on the short side is the VIOLENT-WHIPSAW column: −$20 to −$33 per signal at every single cell and every single R from 0.5 to 6.0. No exit tuning rescues that segment; only not trading it does.

And the engine underneath is real. The 55s veto against its no-veto control on the same tape: abs_veto_55s +$2,668 on 238 fires (+$11.2/trade) vs thrust_loose +$130.5 on 424 fires (+$0.3/trade) — and this week specifically, +$1,328 vs −$235.5. Nobody should read this section as "abs_veto is broken". The gate is the best thing on the board. Its arming policy is what leaked.

The one-sentence answer. abs_veto_long is live, it has been live for a week, and the +$59.5-vs-+$776 gap is not an exit problem or a slot-split problem — it is $500 of signals the router never let it take, $107 of window mismatch, $273 of a stop that did not fill, and one exit cell (long-side 1.5/2.5) that the tick sweep ranks dead last of eleven. There is nothing to promote; there is a bench rule to invert, one JSON cell to change, and a filter-wiring bug to own.

What this does NOT prove. One week, MNQ-only, PAPER, chop-dominated: the CLEAN-TREND buckets (n=6 long / n=5 short) are unscored, so D2 and D4 are untested on a trend week — a wide Lot B is exactly what a trend week would reward, and A1.0/B1.5 caps it. The R sweep spans 8 trading days of tick coverage (07-23 22:00 onward) and every cell is positive on 8/8 LODO folds, which means the folds separate cells by margin only — treat the ordering as a lead, not a law. The ER-floor counterfactual (§3) is tail-dominated at n=24 and is deliberately not used to argue the floor's value in either direction. And the whole live side of this reconciliation ran under a slate whose ER/ATR filters were silently off, so every live abs_veto number in this report is an unfiltered number.

REV2 · Q3 BIG-TREND at n=5 — the plan to prove it, and by when

PATCH MARKER — this section patches PART 2 · LAB ("The exit ladder"): it corrects the n in that section's ★ CRITICAL caveat callout and in the grind (momentum) / BIG-TREND (5) row of its ladder table, withdraws that section's refusal of the MNQ tick archive as factually wrong, and replaces its "must be proven on the next genuine trend week" hand-wave with a dated, owned acquisition plan. It extends REV2 · Q0 (the Monday/Saturday card) with three new dated items. Everything below is computed from scratchpad/exit_ladder_rows.pkl (the 4,553-signal lab), data/capture.db, data/shadow.db, alphabot2/data/ticks.db and alphabot2/data/alphabot.db.

Garrath — you are right that the report left this open, and you are right that it was the worst thing to leave open. But before the plan, two corrections, because the caveat you are quoting is itself wrong in two places. First: the BIG-TREND cell is not n=5. Five is the count of live shadow grind_fast entries that landed in an ER≥0.50 minute (5 in 12 sessions). The tick-tape replay cell that the ladder verdict was actually computed on is n=24. Part 2 · LAB quoted the live number in a caveat about the replay number. Second: the refusal of the MNQ archive was wrong on the facts — and worse, the lab had already used it. The lab tape starts 2026-07-05, not 07-15; eleven of the twenty-four BIG-TREND entries come from that archive stretch. The refusal reads well and is false. Third, and the reason this matters more than either: the unproven rung is not a proposal — it is already live. grind_long has no entry in exit_overrides.json, so it inherits the built-in BIG-RUN default, which is literally Lot A @ 2.5R + Lot B wide lock-chandelier. The desk has been riding the n=24 guess since the scale-out slate went in. This is not a stone left unturned; it is a live position.

The one-paragraph answer

Grade date: Friday 2026-08-21. Hard backstop: Friday 2026-09-11. Owner: Claude (durable router + Friday max-depth lab); operator sign-off on the freeze only. The grading n is 39 paired BIG-TREND entries — and that is a computed number, not a round one: the observed paired advantage of the RIDE exit over the tight scalp is +$79.4/trade with a paired sd of $247, so 2σ separation needs n = 39 (n = 38 at the plateau centre A3.0+WIDE). We are at 24. The remaining 15 come from three sources in this order: (1) extend the tape backwards — alphabot2/data/alphabot.db bar_history holds 334,818 MNQ 5-second bars covering 2026-06-19→07-17, giving 10 full sessions no current tape has, and those sessions are exactly the missing trend (MNQ fell 1,807 points 06-22→06-26; |net|/range ≥ 0.59 on 7 of 11 days) — expected +10 entries (range 8–13), delivered this weekend; (2) re-price all 24 existing entries at 250 ms instead of 5 s — both tick sources between them cover every one of the 12 BIG-TREND entry days, so this costs nothing but compute and it is the honest tier; (3) live-forward tops up the last 3–5 at the measured shadow rate of 0.42 BIG-TREND entries/session, which is 8–12 sessions. Live-forward alone would take until 2026-09-21 all-sides and would never finish on grind_long alone (2 long BIG entries in 12 sessions → ~150 sessions). That is why the plan is tape-first. The MES option is worth nothing for n — the MES archive spans 07-05→07-17, the identical calendar days as the MNQ archive, so it adds zero new sessions; it is retained only as a falsification cross-check.

Read this honestly

The rung is directionally supported and magnitudinally unproven, and those are different verdicts. Supported: wide beats tight on 95% of bootstrap resamples, there is a real parameter plateau (2.5R / 3.0R / 3.5R + wide all land in +$72–84, so the answer is not a knife-edge), both time-halves agree in sign and roughly in size, and no single session carries it (LOO-worst is still +$34 to +$51). Unproven: strip the three best trades and +$62 becomes +$10. Eighty-four percent of the advantage lives in 3 of 24 entries. That is not a reason to disbelieve it — a ride exit is supposed to make its money in the top decile, and the median R-MFE of 4.26 with a p90 of 11.8 says the tail is genuinely there. It is a reason to refuse to call it proven at n=24, which is exactly what the sweep's own robustness filter did when it returned best = None for this cell.

Fact table 4 — why the back-extension is the right tape (and not just more tape)

The V7 tape is chop-dominated because of when it starts. The ten sessions immediately before it are the trend the desk has never been measured on: a 1,807-point MNQ decline 06-22→06-26 (high 30,967.75 → low 29,160.00), followed by two clean directional legs of +662 and −460 points on 06-29→07-01. |net|/range is the day-level trend purity (1.00 = a straight line).

Two caveats, stated up front so nobody has to find them later. (1) 06-19 is dropped — it is expiry day for the Jun'26 contract and sits on the roll boundary; the tape starts 06-22. Continuity across the seam is clean (06-19 high 30,705 → 06-22 range 30,348–30,968), and continuity at the other seam is exact: the last archive MNQ tick is 29,623.00 at 15:35:29.973 and the first capture 5s bar opens 29,622.75 at 15:35:30 — one tick apart, correlation 0.9998 across 2,925 overlapping minutes. Same instrument, same front month. That is the evidence that kills Part 2 · LAB's "different contract era" objection. (2) The pre-07-05 stretch is 5-second bars only — there are no ticks before 07-05 anywhere on this machine. Per [[backtest-on-5s-250ms-persist-all]], 5s repricing showed an 87% flush-loss rate versus 250 ms, and it biases in favour of wide stops — precisely the thing under test. So the back-extension is tier-2 evidence: it may confirm or refute the DIRECTION, it may not set the size. Which is exactly why Track B exists.

The pre-registered PASS test — written now, so it cannot be moved later

The RIDE rung is declared PROVEN for grind_long | BIG-TREND only if all six hold on the graded sample:

1. n ≥ 39 paired BIG-TREND entries (derived: paired sd $247 ÷ diff $79.4, 2σ).  2. mean(RIDE − tight scalp) > 0 with bootstrap P > 0.95.  3. strip-best-3 paired diff > 0 — currently +$10.1, the weakest link and the one most likely to fail.  4. leave-one-session-out worst paired diff ≥ 0.  5. sign agreement across both time-halves (pre-07-16 / post-07-16) and across the 5s tier and the 250 ms tier.  6. the headline number is measured at 250 ms, with the 5s number reported alongside as the optimism gap.

On FAIL: grind_long drops to the proven MED-TREND exit and the RIDE rung is retired as a myth — note this is a real change, not a no-op, because the wide default is live today. On still-short at the backstop: same action. The asymmetry from [[router-benching-lessons]] applies to exits too — a wrongly-tight exit costs missed tail; a wrongly-wide exit costs give-back on every trade that never runs, and 62% of these entries lose money even with the wide exit (WIDEx2 win rate on the cell: 38%).

The thing this exercise found that nobody asked for

Freezing the BIG-TREND cell exposes what the rest of the ladder is doing, because grind_long's A@2.5R+wide default applies in every regime, not just BIG-TREND. On the current tape that default is leaving money in three of the four rungs:

The MED-TREND row is the one with n on its side: 326 entries saying the tight chandelier beats the live wide default by $9.11 a trade. That is a bigger, better-evidenced prize than the BIG-TREND question you asked about — and unlike BIG-TREND it does not need a single new day of tape. It is not in this section's scope to action, but it should not wait for 08-21 either; it belongs in the Saturday 2026-08-01 column of REV2 · Q0, and it is the reason the freeze in Track C should be regime-conditional the moment the router can express that.

Straight answer to the question as asked

(a) The live-forward trigger: first Paris session with a ≥15-minute contiguous 30-min-ER ≥ 0.50 leg inside 13:00–21:00 UTC and grind_long=on; base rate 3 of 20 sessions; the BIG-TREND cell is pre-frozen at {"grind_long":{"a_r":3.0,"b":"wide"}} from Monday 2026-08-03 and any edit to it voids the test. (b) The matching-instrument option: the MNQ-before-07-15 idea is already spent — capture.db does not reach back, and the ticks.db archive that does is already 11 of the 24; the MES 07-12→07-17 window adds zero new sessions because it covers the same calendar days, so it is demoted to a falsification cross-check. The real matching-instrument answer is one nobody had found: 10 untouched MNQ sessions in alphabot.db bar_history, 06-22→07-03, containing the 1,807-point trend the V7 tape is missing. (c) The grading n: 39, derived from the observed paired sd, not chosen. Date: Friday 2026-08-21, hard backstop Friday 2026-09-11. Owner: Claude, on the durable router tick and the Friday max-depth lab, with your sign-off required only on the Monday freeze.

REV2 · Q4 The router's own estimator says it cost −$1,216 this week. Does it earn its keep or not?

Garrath — you caught a real one. Part 2.5 and Part 2.6 both print net router value −$1,216 and both conclude "earning its keep", and the defence in between ("those winners never ran; shadow overstates winners") is asserted and never sized. That is a verdict that does not follow its own numbers, and you were right to poke it. So I sized it. Two things are wrong with the −$1,216: (1) 60% of it is the dual-slot A/B double-count the report admits to but never removes — stripping it takes the week to −$440; (2) the estimator prices zero slippage on a book of 353 modelled round trips, and because the router blocks winners and losers, any execution drag at all moves the number up — breakeven is $1.25 per round trip, or 2.5 MNQ ticks. The standing memory's own floor (live losses ≥1.1× modelled) implies ~8 ticks, which puts the week at +$940.

But the honest answer is not "so it is earning its keep." It is that the blended number is the wrong statistic, because its two halves have opposite signs and each half is individually robust. Benching in CHOP earns +$1,088. Benching in TREND_UP costs −$1,380. The first is positive before any haircut and survives every leave-one-day-out fold; the second needs a 52% winner haircut to break even, which is outside any defensible band. Net-net the machine is a coin flip; the policy inside it is half-proven and half-broken. One number, stated band, below.

Honesty banner. Every figure below is re-derived from source, not lifted from the nightly JSONs: 37,160 SUPPRESSED-OPEN lines from the gazbot7-tournament journal for Paris 07-27…07-31, repriced tick-honest against capture.db with the same estimator the nightly uses (exit_scalp 2R target / 1-ATR stop, VPP $2.00, fee $1.50/RT), and regime-tagged by replaying direction_router.replay_marks at each signal. My re-derivation of the published method reproduces it to $115 on $16.8k of gross (0.7%) — see the reconciliation row — so the corrections below are corrections, not a different model. Limits stated plainly: n=353 blocked signals, 5 days, one summer chop-dominated week, MNQ-only, PAPER. Every blocked trade never ran — "missed" is a modelled ceiling throughout. The CLEAN-TREND (n=38), DEAD-CHOP (n=3) and US_OPEN30 (n=6) buckets are reported but not scored. And the regime tags come from the router's own replay, so this is a self-consistent audit ("given what the router believed, did the bench pay?"), not an independent regime oracle.

1 · Reconciliation — am I even looking at the same number?

Before correcting anything, reproduce it. Same journal, same repricer, same 120-second signal dedup, A/B slots counted separately exactly as router_nightly.py counts them:

Sourceblocked signalssavedmissednet router value
Published — Part 2.5 §3 & Part 2.6 §3—$15,788$17,003−$1,216
My re-derivation, identical method652$15,730$16,832−$1,101
delta—−$58−$171$115

Read: $115 on $16.8k of gross = 0.7%, and it is drift in the right direction — capture.db has backfilled ticks since the nightlies ran, so a handful of signals now reprice with more tape. The published number is real and I can reproduce it. Everything below is a correction to it, not a competing estimate.

2 · Error 1 — the dual-slot A/B double-count (worth $661, i.e. 60% of the deficit)

Part 2.6 says the A/B slots "double-count one signal" and then leaves them in the total. They do double-count, and the proof is that the two legs are byte-identical in the nightly's own output. From data/router_nightly/2026-07-31.json:

gatenwonwon_pnllostlost_pnl
grind_long_A11646$2,633.570−$2,228.5
grind_long_B11646$2,633.570−$2,228.5

Why they are identical, mechanically: router_nightly.reprice_scalp() applies exit_scalp(target_r=2.0, stop_atr_mult=1.0) to every blocked signal regardless of slot suffix. It does not model Lot B's chandelier at all. So the estimator takes one suppressed signal, reprices it twice under the same exit, and books both copies into saved and missed. The fix is to merge the raw journal events per base gate before the 120-second dedup, so one suppressed signal counts once:

Week 07-27…07-31signalssavedmissednet
A/B counted separately (as published)652$15,730$16,832−$1,101
A/B merged — one signal, counted once353$8,644$9,084−$440
duplicate share stripped−46%−45%−46%$661 recovered

Read: ~46% of both columns was a duplicate, and because the columns are near-equal the duplication does not cancel — it scales the gap too. Stripping it accounts for $661 of the $1,216, i.e. 60% of the deficit was a bookkeeping artifact. The A/B pairs appear from 07-29, the day the scale-out slate went live — the same slate REV2·Q1/Q2 found silently voided the ER/ATR floors. Same root cause, second symptom. Residual known bias I am not fixing: even the merged number still prices Lot B under Lot A's 2R scalp exit. Direction of that error is unknown and it is not claimed as support for the router.

3 · Error 2 — the estimator prices zero slippage, and the router blocks both sides

The repricer enters at the signal's touch price and exits at the first tick that crosses 2R or the 1-ATR stop. Fees are modelled ($1.50/RT); slippage is modelled at exactly zero on both legs. Real fills are worse than the touch on entry and exit — this is the standing memory [[shadow-sim-understates-losses]] (live losses 1.1–3.1× modelled) and [[execution-cost-autopsy-stage1]] (measured stop-slippage ≈$1,200 leak).

The key mechanical point the report never makes: the router blocked 221 would-be losers and 132 would-be winners (mean modelled loss $39.12, mean modelled win $68.82). A per-round-trip execution drag δ therefore deepens the losses it saved you from and shrinks the winners it cost you. Both move the same way. So:

net(δ) = −$440 + δ × 353   →   breakeven at δ = $1.25 / round trip = 2.5 MNQ ticks

One MNQ tick = 0.25 index points = $0.50 at the desk's $2.00/pt. So the entire −$1,216 headline rests on the claim that a blocked round trip suffers less than three ticks of total slippage across entry, target and stop. Here is the ladder, in physical units, with the loss-multiplier each drag implies against the modelled mean loss of $39.12:

MNQ ticks / RTδ ($/RT)implied loss mult.net router valuenote
0$0.001.00×−$440the estimator's own assumption
2$1.001.03×−$86
2.5$1.251.03×$0breakeven
4$2.001.05×+$266
7.8$3.911.10×+$940the memory band's FLOOR
16$8.001.20×+$2,384
164$82.153.10×+$28,600REJECTED — see below
I am explicitly rejecting the top of my own memory band. [[shadow-sim-understates-losses]] says 1.1–3.1×, but that band was measured on realised trades with real holding profiles. Applied as a linear per-RT drag to 353 modelled signals it produces +$28.6k, which is obvious nonsense and would be exactly the kind of unsized hand-wave this section exists to kill. I use the FLOOR of the band and nothing above it. If the defence only works at the top of a band, it is not a defence.

The haircut form of the same question — winners discounted by h, saved column held fixed (the most conservative framing, it takes from one side only):

winner haircut h0%4.8%10%15%20%30%
net (A/B stripped)−$440$0+$469+$923+$1,377+$2,286
net (as published, A/B in)−$1,216−$401+$484+$1,334+$2,185+$3,885

→ Direct answer to the question you asked: with the double-count stripped, −$1,216 flips positive at a 4.8% winner haircut. On the published numbers as printed, it flips at 7.1%. Both are low bars — but "low bar" is a directional argument again, which is why the number alone is not the verdict. Section 4 is.

4 · The verdict the blend was hiding — segment it and the two halves disagree

Per the backtest discipline: never score a blended cross-tape number. Splitting the 353 de-duplicated blocked signals by regime at signal (the router's own replay) is where the whole question resolves:

regime @ signalnsavedmissednethaircut to flipverdict
CHOP245$6,174$5,087+$1,088none needed✅ EARNS ITS KEEP positive at h=0
TREND_DOWN44$1,216$1,364−$14810.8%marginal, low n
TREND_UP64$1,253$2,633−$1,38052.4%❌ DOES NOT outside any band
blend353$8,644$9,084−$4404.8%the number that hid both

The whole deficit is one segment. Benching in CHOP — the router's core defensive job — is worth +$1,088 before any haircut at all, no execution-drag assumption required. Benching during TREND_UP costs −$1,380 and would need the shadow to be overstating winners by 52% to break even, which is far outside anything the memory band supports. That is [[us-open-dont-bench-trend-rider]] and the two ❌ BAD ledger calls, finally with a price tag on them.

Read the violent-whipsaw row. That is Friday. Benching the faders into ATR-52/62 violence was worth +$408; benching the momentum gates in the same tape cost −$869. Part 2.6 §6 called the Friday defensive trim "shadow-vindicated" — half right. The fader half was vindicated; the momentum half was the most expensive segment of the week.

By session (US open = 13:30 UTC): OVERNIGHT_EU n=216, −$93. US_SESSION n=131, −$97. US_OPEN30 (13:00–13:30 UTC) n=6, −$249 — and 5 of the 6 blocked signals would have won. n=6 so it is reported, not scored, but it is the sharpest single illustration of the banked rule: benching across the open is where the router pays the most per decision.

5 · Robustness — which of these two conclusions actually survives?

The discipline demands leave-one-day-out and strip-the-best-trades on every claim. Here both, run on the blend and on the same book with the TREND_UP benches removed:

testblended netnet, TREND_UP benches removed
all 5 days−$440+$940
LOO — drop 07-27−$358+$1,021
LOO — drop 07-28−$342+$958
LOO — drop 07-29+$230+$640
LOO — drop 07-30−$804+$608
LOO — drop 07-31−$484+$533
strip top & bottom 3 trades−$226+$753
strip top & bottom 5 trades−$72+$591
strip top & bottom 10 trades+$268+$1,008
verdictSIGN FLIPS (−$804…+$268)
not robust — do not quote it		POSITIVE IN ALL 9 FOLDS (+$533…+$1,021)
robust

This is the finding. The blended router-value number changes sign depending on which single day you drop — it has no statistical standing in either direction, and neither Part 2.5's "−$1,216, over-benched" nor Part 2.6's "yes, earning its keep" is supportable from it. What is robust is the split: remove the TREND_UP benches and the week is positive in every leave-one-day-out fold and every trade-stripping fold, at zero haircut, with no execution-drag assumption whatsoever.

Sized policy fix (all at h=0, no drag assumed):

policysignalsweek netΔ vs as-run
as run353−$440—
stop benching momentum in TREND_UP (56 signals)297+$1,175+$1,615
stop benching anything in TREND_UP (64 signals)289+$940+$1,380

6 · Error 3 — the leakage column, which neither section reconciled

Part 2.5 prints week leakage −$1,083. Part 2.6 prints "$0 (durable days)" on the same row of the same table. Both are true in their own frame (−$456/−$398/−$229 on 27/28/29, $0 on 30/31), but the report never says so, and a reader comparing the two sections sees a contradiction. Worse, sitting in the same table as saved/missed it invites being netted in. It must not be.

  • Leakage is the only realised-money number in that whole table. saved and missed are modelled counterfactuals on trades that never ran; leakage is real losers that really fired. Do not present them side by side without saying which is which.
  • Leakage is not a router cost line. It counts managed-gate losers that fired while the gate was ON. The no-router baseline has those same losers. Subtracting it from router value would triple-count the router's failures — a third bookkeeping error on top of the two above.
  • What it is: an under-benching diagnostic. And it points the opposite way to the over-benching story both sections lead with — the week's realised evidence says the router was too loose early (−$1,083 through Tuesday) and got tighter, not that it was uniformly trigger-happy.

7 · One number, with a band

You asked for a number instead of a directional argument. Here it is.

Router net value · Paris 07-27…07-31 · A/B-stripped · tick-honestvalue
point estimate, zero execution drag (the estimator's own assumption)−$440
point estimate, memory-floor drag (1.1× losses ⇒ $3.91/RT ≈ 7.8 ticks)+$940
★ STATED VALUE — midpoint with band+$250  [ −$440 , +$940 ]
for comparison — the figure Parts 2.5 & 2.6 published−$1,216
for comparison — the desk's actual realised P&L, same week+$625
The verdict, stated properly:

1. −$1,216 is wrong. $661 of it is the dual-slot A/B double-count; the rest sits on a zero-slippage assumption that breaks at 2.5 MNQ ticks per round trip. Corrected, the week is +$250, band −$440 to +$940.

2. "Earning its keep" was also wrong — as stated. Not because the sign is negative, but because the blended number flips sign on leave-one-day-out (−$804 to +$230). It cannot support a verdict in either direction. Part 2.6 §8's "Yes" and Part 2.5's "two things pull the other way, hard" were both reasoning from a statistic with no standing. On aggregate P&L this week the router is indistinguishable from zero.

3. The real answer is a split verdict, and both halves ARE robust:
  • Defence — benching faders and benching into CHOP: EARNS ITS KEEP. +$1,088 (CHOP) / +$613 (faders), positive before any haircut, robust to every fold.
  • Offence — benching momentum in TREND_UP: DOES NOT. −$1,380, needing a 52% winner haircut to break even.

4. So the honest answer to "does it earn its keep" is: half of it does, decisively, and the other half is eating the proceeds. Fixing the one segment is worth +$1,615/week at zero haircut and is positive in all nine robustness folds — which makes "stop benching aligned momentum in a confirmed TREND_UP" the highest-value, best-evidenced change in this entire report, and it needs no new build: it is the rule already banked as [[us-open-dont-bench-trend-rider]], simply not being enforced.

5. Caveat that must travel with the number: one week, n=353 modelled signals, chop-dominated summer tape, MNQ-only, PAPER, and the regime tags are the router's own. The segment split is the robust finding; the ±$690 band on the headline is honest width, not false precision. Re-run after a genuine trend week before sizing anything off the TREND_UP figure.

REV3 · TREND_UP Q4 says arming momentum in TREND_UP is worth +$1,615/week. I tried to kill it. It mostly died.

Garrath — Q4 called this "the highest-value, best-evidenced change in this entire report." I re-derived it from source, independently, and tried to break it. The arithmetic is right and I reproduce it: +$1,644 on my numbers vs Q4's +$1,615. But three things it did not test take it apart. (1) The estimator books every deduped signal as a full trade even when the same gate is already in one — the temporal twin of the A/B double-count Q4 itself caught. Fixing it: +$1,799 → +$767. (2) The TREND_UP label is the carrier, and it does not survive independence: under a textbook ADX(14) tag the segment is worth +$574 and is statistically indistinguishable from a random subset of the same book (p=0.56); under a 60-min regression tag it is −$276 and significantly WORSE than random (z=−2.56). (3) The money is not trend money. 52% of it comes from two grind_long episodes on 07-29 — a day MNQ fell 702 points at day-ER 0.72, i.e. momentum longs buying bounces in the week's cleanest DOWN trend, which the desk's own day-bias rule says to bench. The one genuinely clean UP day of the week (07-30, +1,126pt, day-ER 0.93) contributes $349 of $1,799.

The number: REDUCED from +$1,615 to a best-case +$767/week, and to +$158 on n=4 signals once the rule is written so it cannot buy dips in a downtrend. REFUTED as a "trend" finding. Recommendation: do not ship it as an arming rule. It points the expensive direction on a tape that contains no confirmed trend to test it on — by my own operational definition, a confirmed trend was live for 3.3% of the week, 22 episodes, median 6 minutes each. There is nothing here to ride. What I do recommend, and what is worth more than the whole debate, is in §7: all six gates are currently PINNED ON, so the durable router cannot bench anything at all — the proven half of the machine is switched off right now.

Honesty banner. Independent re-derivation, not a re-read of Q4. Source = the same 37,160 SUPPRESSED-OPEN lines from the gazbot7-tournament journal, Paris 07-27…07-31, parsed fresh; every reprice on capture.db 250ms ticks (8,448,165 ticks loaded), never 1-min bars; DuckDB + pandas throughout. ATR and the 2R/1-ATR exit match router_nightly.reprice_scalp() exactly so the numbers are comparable. Every blocked trade never ran — every dollar below is a modelled CEILING, and live losses run 1.1–3.1× modelled [[shadow-sim-understates-losses]]. n=355 merged signals, 5 days, one chop-dominated summer week, MNQ-only, PAPER. Where I disagree with Q4 I show both numbers. Working files: /tmp/…/rev3/derive.py, analyse{,2,…,7}.py. Read-only: nothing was applied.

2 · Kill shot #1 — the estimator books trades a real gate could never have taken

Q4 stripped the A/B duplicate. It left the temporal duplicate. router_nightly.py dedups suppressions at 120 seconds and then prices every survivor as a complete round trip — even when the previous signal on that same gate is still notionally open. Median modelled hold is minutes to tens of minutes; the dedup window is two. A real gate holds one position at a time.

Correction: walk each gate's signals in time order, and skip any that arrives before the previous one has exited (using that trade's own tick-derived exit time).

book (aligned momentum LONG, router TREND_UP)nnetwin%$/tradeLOO worst foldstrip best 3strip best 5
as Q4 counts it — every deduped signal is a trade56+$1,79961%+$32.1—+$1,304+$978
one open position per gate (non-overlap)32+$76756%+$24.0+$276+$276+$85
and including the counter-side (all momentum, as Q4 words it)38+$61250%+$16.1+$121+$122−$70

Read: −57% from a bookkeeping fix of exactly the species Q4 itself found. And Q4's own wording — "stop benching momentum in TREND_UP" — includes abs_veto_short, i.e. arming a SHORT gate inside an up-trend. That sub-slice is n=6, −$154, 17% win. Counter-side momentum in a trend loses money and should never have been in the number. Aligned-only is the only defensible version.

3 · Kill shot #2 — the finding lives or dies on whose definition of TREND_UP

Q4 flags the circularity in its own honesty banner and then scores against the router's tag anyway. So I tagged the same 355 signals four more ways, none of which touches direction_router, and ran a placebo: if the TREND_UP label carries no information, a random same-size subset of the aligned momentum book should do as well. 20,000 draws.

regime oracle for TREND_UP% of weeknnet (raw)win%$/sigvs randompverdict

Read this row by row. The router's own ER-based tag picks a subset worth $32/signal against a random-subset mean of $7.6/signal — a real, significant selection (z=+3.60). No other formulation reproduces it. The obvious defence is "the others use a longer horizon" — so I built one at the same 30-minute horizon with the same ±30pt threshold, swapping Kaufman ER for regression R². It finds $0.6/signal. The discriminator is not the horizon. It is the specific ER_TREND=0.20 / NET_MIN=30 / HOLD=2 triple — and those three knobs were tuned on 07-20…07-26, the week immediately before this one.

The comparison that should end the argument: under the two independent tags, the TREND_UP bucket is not even the best bucket.

Under both independent oracles the CHOP bucket is where the blocked momentum longs would have made money — which is not a case for arming momentum in trends, it is a case that this estimator likes momentum longs everywhere this week: the whole aligned momentum-LONG blocked book is +$2,078 across all 274 signals in all regimes. A finding that says "you should never have benched a momentum long anywhere, all week" is not a TREND_UP finding. It is a statement about the estimator's optimism and about a week that closed 217 points lower but bounced hard.

4 · Kill shot #3 — it is not trend money. It is bounce money on the biggest DOWN day.

07-27, 07-280$0——−291 / −251pt0.35 / 0.40no TREND_UP signals at all

The one clean up-trend day of the week — the exact tape [[us-open-dont-bench-trend-rider]] was written for — contributes $349 of $1,799 (19%). The two biggest episodes are both grind_long on 07-29:

episodesignalsnetwindow (UTC)priceday context
grind_long#2@07-295+$57018:40 → 18:4927868 → 28025day 27962 → 27260 (−702pt)
grind_long#1@07-294+$37116:52 → 16:5827552 → 27579same day, price 400pt below the open
grind_long#10@07-314+$35316:26 → 16:5628312 → 28376day-ER 0.05, ATR-52 whipsaw

Those top two are +$941 = 52% of the entire claim, and both are momentum longs bought into bounces while MNQ was 400–700 points below its open on the week's cleanest down day. The framework's own rule is day-bias DOWN≤−40 → bench longs. 34% of the claimed money ($614 over 10 signals) was earned with the day-bias at or below −40. To bank Q4's +$1,615 the router would have to override the day-bias rule — which Q4 never says and nobody has asked for.

Two more concentration cuts, same direction:

  • Episodes, not signals. 56 signals collapse to 21 episodes (same gate, gaps <30 min). Top-1 episode = 35% of the total; top-3 = 79%. Strip the best three episodes and $1,799 becomes +$350. Effective n is ~20, not 56.
  • ER at the moment of firing. The money sits in the lowest ER bucket: ER30 ≤ 0.20 → n=32, +$1,351 (75% of the claim); ER30 ≥ 0.35 → n=9, +$6. Because replay_marks carries HOLD=2 hysteresis on a 15-min grid, a "TREND_UP" label at signal time can be up to half an hour stale. 57% of the claimed signals fired while the tape was, on its own instantaneous read, chop. That is [[rearm-momentum-needs-er-climb-not-delta-blip]] with a price tag on the wrong side of the ledger.

5 · What I could NOT kill — the estimator itself, and the fader half

Two adversarial tests came back in Q4's favour and I report them straight.

(a) The estimator is not systematically optimistic. Direct calibration against the same week's realised trades:

momentum gates, 07-27…07-31nnetwin%$/trade
REALISED (actually ran, real exits, real fills)141+$60235%+$4.3
MODELLED (blocked, 2R/1-ATR on 250ms ticks)326+$1,28439%+$3.9

Modelled is $0.3/trade WORSE than realised. So the reduction in §2–§4 is not a slippage argument and I am not making one. Consistent with that, the drag maths runs Q4's way: arming means taking these trades, so net = Σpnl − δ·n, and on the router tag the aligned book breaks even only at δ=$24/RT ≈ 48 MNQ ticks. Slippage does not kill this. The tag and the overlap do.

(b) Exit choice is not the artefact. Repriced on the same ticks under six exits — the finding is stable in shape, which means the 2R/1-ATR estimator is not flattering it:

exit variant (router tag, aligned LONG, n=56)netwin%$/trade

2R / 1.5-ATR wider stop+$2,46875%+$44.1
chandelier 3.5→0.5k / 1-ATR stop+$2,49661%+$44.6

(c) And a correction that runs AGAINST Q4's other half. Q4 states the defence as "+$1,088 in CHOP, robust to every fold." The CHOP claim is tag-dependent in exactly the same way the TREND_UP claim is:

defensive cutrouter tagADX tagOLS-60 tagverdict
bench into CHOP — router value, non-overlap+$612+$187−$382tag-FRAGILE, same flaw
bench the FADERS — router value, non-overlap+$810 (n=25) — a mechanism cut, identical under every tag✅ the one robust claim

So the surviving half of Q4 is narrower than Q4 says. "Benching faders earns its keep" holds under any definition of anything — exhaustion_short alone: 12 of 12 blocked signals would have lost, +$648. "Benching into CHOP earns its keep" only holds if you accept the router's own definition of chop.

6 · Sized ladder — what any version of this rule is actually worth

Every row is "arm ALIGNED long momentum when <condition>", non-overlap, at h=0 with no drag assumed, on this one week. LOO = worst leave-one-Paris-day-out fold. δbe = per-round-trip slippage that zeroes it.

candidate rulenweek netwin%$/tradeLOO worststrip best 3strip best 5δbe
Q4 as written — router TREND_UP, ALL momentum38+$61250%+$16.1+$121+$122−$70$16.1
aligned-only — router TREND_UP + LONG32+$76756%+$24.0+$276+$276+$85$24.0
+ day-bias UP (price ≥ day open +40) — framework-consistent23+$42257%+$18.4+$230+$91−$34$18.4
+ day-bias UP + ADX(14) confirm17+$44659%+$26.2+$253+$88−$36$26.2
+ day-bias UP + ER30≥0.35 (the grind floor the desk already uses)4+$15850%+$39.5−$34−$58$0$39.5

US session only (13:30–20:00z) + day-bias UP4+$16250%+$40.6−$2−$50$0$40.6

Read the shape, not any single row. Every step that makes the rule more consistent with the desk's existing framework makes it smaller, and every version fails at least one robustness cut. The loosest version (Q4's own wording) is the one that dies fastest under strip-best-5. The tightest versions are n=4 for the entire week.

Q4 says the fix is "already banked as [[us-open-dont-bench-trend-rider]], simply not being enforced." That is not what the banked rule says. The banked rule is narrow: on a cleanly-trending day, hold the aligned trend-rider through open stop-outs; bench only if trend STRUCTURE breaks. It is a rule about not panicking on flushes. Q4's version is a rule about arming momentum for 18% of the clock on a 30-min ER≥0.20 read. Those are different rules, and the ledger already grades the boundary between them: 07-30 13:36 (❌ BAD, benched the rider on open noise in a clean trend) versus 07-31 13:40 (✅ GOOD, benched grind after the thrust fully reversed). The banked rule is being enforced correctly. Q4 is proposing a new and much wider one.

7 · The asymmetry, honestly — and the thing that actually matters this week

The standing rule is that wrongly-benched is cheap (missed trades) and wrongly-armed is expensive (real churn losses), so when unsure, sit benched one more tick. This change points the expensive way. Everything about the evidence says respect the asymmetry:

what the asymmetry test asksanswer from this week's tape
Is the effect robust to how you define the condition?❌ No — significant under one tag, null under a second, negative under a third.
Is it robust to leave-one-day-out?❌ Not on the framework-consistent versions (LOO −$34); marginally yes only on the loosest.
Is it robust to stripping the best few?❌ Strip 3 episodes: $1,799 → $350. Strip best 5 signals: Q4's own wording → −$70.
Is the tape representative of the condition being armed for?❌ No. A confirmed trend was live 3.3% of the week — 22 episodes, median 6 minutes. There was nothing to ride.
Does the wrongly-armed failure mode already have a body count?❌ Yes. Ledger 07-31: enable-all at the open → grind bought the roll-over −$370; two afternoon re-arms into a "calm" ER-0.24–0.30 recovery churned −$245. Both would be more likely under Q4's rule, because 57% of its signals fire at ER30 ≤ 0.20.
What is the upside if it is right?+$400–$770/week modelled ceiling, on a book whose realised comparator did $4.3/trade.

Verdict: STAGE IT, do not ship it. A change that arms a churner in more conditions, justified by a segment that inverts when you change the definition of the segment, on a week with no confirmed trend in it, is precisely the trade the asymmetry rule exists to refuse. The cost of waiting is a modelled few hundred dollars a week that may not exist; the cost of being wrong is measured, repeatedly, in the ledger.

★ And the operational finding that is worth more than this entire debate. As of right now:

live state, 2026-08-01valueconsequence
PINNED in scripts/router_tick_durable.pyfrozenset({all 6 gates})the durable tick cannot bench ANY gate
data/gate_switches.envall 6 = ondesk fully armed in every regime
comment in the file"★US-OPEN OVERRIDE 2026-07-31 … Revert to frozenset({"exhaustion_short"}) once the open settles."

The open settled two days ago. The proven half of the router — the fader bench, +$810/week, the only claim in Q4 that survives every test I could throw at it — is currently switched off, and so is the CHOP bench. Q4's recommendation is to arm momentum in more conditions; the desk is already in the maximally-armed state, by accident. Before any argument about arming more, the pin should come off so the machine can do the part it demonstrably earns. That is a one-line revert, it points the CHEAP direction, and it needs no new evidence.

8 · The staged path — how to earn this finding instead of assuming it

  1. Un-pin first (today). PINNED = frozenset({"exhaustion_short"}) — restore the fade-scalp trial's protection only, and let the durable tick resume benching. Reinstates the +$810 fader defence. No new build, no new risk, cheap direction.
  2. Instrument, do not arm. Add nothing to the prompt. Instead extend router_nightly.py so it (a) merges A/B before dedup, (b) enforces one-open-position-per-gate, and (c) tags each blocked signal with ADX and the router replay. Every night thereafter produces the honest version of this table for free. Until that lands, every "router value" number in the Friday report carries a ~2× counting inflation.
  3. Wait for the tape. The whole question is unanswerable on a week containing 224 minutes of confirmed trend. Re-run this exact ladder after a week with a real trend day — the deciding test is whether the ADX-confirmed and router-only versions converge. If they do, the effect is real and worth arming. If they keep disagreeing, the router's ER triple is overfit and should be retuned, not obeyed.
  4. If the operator wants something in force now, the only asymmetry-safe change is a TIGHTENING, and it is written out below. It does not arm anything new. It (i) defines "confirmed trend" operationally so no future tick can re-arm on a 30-minute-stale label, (ii) forbids arming the counter-side momentum gate inside a confirmed trend (measured: −$154, 17% win), and (iii) restates the banked open rule in its narrow, correct form. It is PREPARED, NOT APPLIED.
PREPARED, NOT APPLIED — conservative patch to scripts/router_tick_durable.py. This is not Q4's change. Q4's change is declined. This one is a tightening: it can only cause a gate to be benched in situations where it might previously have been armed, plus one narrow hold-through-flushes clause that already exists as a banked rule. The chop/fader benching wording is untouched by construction — the insert sits after it and says so explicitly.

Insert point — immediately after the existing sentence ending "...(not a delta-blip). " and before "abs_veto_long is veto-protected...", inside the prompt = ( literal:

        "★ CONFIRMED-TREND TEST — apply this whenever you would hold or re-arm an ALIGNED momentum gate. "
        "It does NOT loosen the chop/fader benching above (that is the proven half; leave it exactly as is). "
        "A trend counts as CONFIRMED only if ALL FOUR are true on THIS tick's desk-view read, never on a "
        "reading from an earlier tick: (1) 30-min ER >= 0.35; (2) 30-min net move >= 60 MNQ points in the "
        "trend direction; (3) ATR >= 14; (4) the same read held on the previous tick too (~10 min). "
        "If any one of the four fails it is NOT a confirmed trend — it is chop, and momentum stays benched, "
        "however directional the last 30 minutes looked. In a CONFIRMED trend: hold the ALIGNED momentum "
        "gate through stop-outs (an open flush is noise — bench it only if the trend STRUCTURE breaks: the "
        "leg fully round-trips, or ER falls back under 0.20), and BENCH the counter-side momentum gate. "
        "Never arm a momentum gate against the day-bias. "

Exact revert: delete the seven inserted string-literal lines. Nothing else in the file changes — no constant, no default, no PINNED edit, no behaviour outside the prompt text. The wrapper is fail-safe either way (any parse failure ⇒ no switch change).

What it would have done this week, measured, not asserted — and this cuts against my own patch, so read it. The four-part test was true for 3.3% of the 1-min clock (1.8% UP, 1.4% DOWN), across 22 episodes of median 6 minutes. As a permission filter it clears 26 of the 274 aligned-long signal-instants (9%), worth −$28 raw / +$86 non-overlap, win 42%, and even that flips negative on two of four leave-one-day-out folds (−$25, −$60) and on strip-best-3 (−$260). The counter-side clause codifies what already happened rather than changing it: those 6 abs_veto_short signals were benched, worth +$154 saved — the clause's job is to stop a future tick arming them, which is exactly what Q4's wording would have done.

So: the tightened rule earns approximately nothing this week either. That is the point, and I would rather say it than sell it. Its value is not money, it is determinism — right now "confirmed TREND_UP" is whatever a headless Claude decides at 03:00 UTC with no operator watching, and 57% of the signals Q4 wants armed fire at ER30 ≤ 0.20. The patch makes the phrase mean one thing. Sell it as a guardrail, size it at $0, and expect the argument about arming to be settled by a future trend week, not by this one.

9 · Bottom line

claimQ4REV3status
Published −$1,216 is wrong; A/B double-count is $661 of it✔✔✅ CONFIRMED

Blended router value has no statistical standing✔✔✅ CONFIRMED — do not quote it
Benching the FADERS earns its keep+$613+$810✅ CONFIRMED and strengthened — the one tag-independent claim
Benching into CHOP earns its keep, robust to every fold+$1,088+$612 / +$187 / −$382⚠ DOWNGRADED — tag-fragile, same flaw as the TREND_UP half
Stop benching momentum in TREND_UP = +$1,615/week, robust in all 9 folds+$1,615+$767 → +$158❌ REDUCED, and REFUTED as a trend finding
"Highest-value, best-evidenced change in the report; needs no new build"✔✘❌ DECLINED — stage it; un-pin the router instead

One paragraph for the plane. Q4 found two real bookkeeping errors and one real segment split, and it deserves credit for all three. But it stopped one correction short: the same estimator that double-counts A and B also double-counts a gate against itself in time, and fixing that halves the headline finding. What is left is significant only under the router's own thirty-minute ER definition of a trend, is null under a textbook ADX and negative under a regression fit at the same horizon, and is half-funded by two clusters of momentum longs bought into bounces on the week's biggest down day. The rule it asks for is not the rule the desk banked. Keep the defence, keep the ledger's asymmetry, take the pin off so the router can defend again, and re-run this the first week the tape gives us a real trend.

REV3 The dead floors — confirmed, re-derived, and the order to ship them in tonight

Garrath — Q2 is right. Every ER and ATR floor on the desk has been a no-op since 2026-07-29 16:20:49 UTC, and I confirmed it independently from source, from the journal, and from the commit dates. But Q2 overstated the size by a factor of 44, and it left the important half undone. Here is the important half. Four of the five floors should never be switched back on. The two ER floors the operator wrote on 07-30 are the two most expensive lines of config on the desk: over eight days of tick-honest replay, grind_long's ER≥0.35 would have thrown away +$2,923 of profit to keep +$83, and abs_veto_long's ER≥0.25 would have thrown away +$640 to keep +$148. Neither has ever run, which is the only reason the desk is up. If you ship the wiring fix tonight and nothing else, you take the four floored gates from 382 signals / +$3,630 down to 103 signals / +$668 — a −$2,962 self-inflicted wound on eight days of tape, about −$370 a day. The ship order below exists to make that impossible.

Honesty banner. Every number is measured, none recalled. Sources: the gazbot7-tournament journal (274,106 lines, 07-20→08-01), data/gazbot7.db, data/shadow.db, and data/capture.db (10,080,959 MNQ ticks + 9,546,332 clean quotes). Every exit is replayed on the 250ms stream, never on a bar. The replay is a clone of the desk's own src/gazbot7/repricer.py and reproduces shadow_real.real_pnl to $0.00 MAE on four of six gates (details §2). Every dollar below is a CEILING — memory [[shadow-sim-understates-losses]] puts live losses at 1.1–3.1× modelled, so I carry a break-even loss multiplier on every recommendation and treat anything under ~1.5× as not really green. The tape is 8 days, one chop-dominated summer regime, MNQ-only, PAPER. My replay is always-armed — it does not model router benching, which in the same window benched 465 signal episodes.

1 · The bug — CONFIRMED, with one correction to Q2

Mechanism, one sentence: tournament.step() passes the raw slot tag to er_blocks() and atr_blocks(), but under the dual-slot scale-out slate every tag carries an _A/_B suffix while ER_FLOOR/ATR_FLOOR are keyed on base names — so the dictionary lookup never matches and every floor silently returns "don't block".

The _base() helper that strips the suffix already exists at tournament.py:86 and is correctly applied three times — to the bench switch (:250), the 55s absorption veto (:303) and the exhaustion confirm (:345). It was simply not applied to the two floors at :139 and :142.

evidencewhat it establishes
sourcetournament.py:139 if er_blocks(slot, er): · :142 if atr_blocks(slot, f.atr): — raw slot. deciders.py:107/173 key on base names. Working tree clean for all three decision-path files; editable install; the running process loaded exactly this code.
cutoverCommit 5b0913b "dual-slot scale-out slate" 07-29 16:18:50 → drop-in /etc/systemd/system/gazbot7-tournament.service.d/scaleout.conf written 16:20:00 → restart 16:20:49 → journal tournament LIVE — slots=['grind_long_A','grind_long_B',…] at 16:20:50.
journalLast suppression line of any kind: 2026-07-29T13:29:04Z. Since the cutover: exactly zero ER or ATR suppression lines in 2 days 18 hours, against a prior run-rate of 1,500–2,700 lines/day. Verified by exhaustive scan, not sampling.
commit datesBoth ER floors were written AFTER the bug. grind_long 0.35 = aa59357, 07-30 10:23:53. abs_veto_long 0.25 = 9767c67, 07-30 10:57:10. Both ~18h after the cutover. The journal contains zero ER gate: grind_long lines in its entire 12-day history. Neither floor has ever blocked a single trade in production.

The one correction. Q2's headline "7,734 suppressions" is a raw log-line count, and the check re-fires every ~1s decision tick for as long as a signal stands. Collapsing consecutive same-gate lines ≤5s apart into episodes:

measureER gateATR gatecombined
raw log lines (Q2's basis)7,7449,08016,824
distinct blocked signal episodes140243383
overstatement factor55×37×44×

So the honest sentence is: the floors were blocking about 48 signal episodes a day across all gates, not thousands — concentrated in grind_long ATR (191 episodes) and the two ER gates (40 each). That is material, but an order of magnitude smaller than the number that has been circulating. The bug is real; its scale was not.

What else the same bug killed, and what it did not. I audited every tag-keyed lookup in the decision path. The bug kills exactly three things: the ER floors, the ATR floors, and — collaterally — the ER_HOLD abs_veto_long spike-filter shadow study (tournament.py:148), which has been recording no data for three days. That last one is observe-only, so no P&L impact, but it means that study is three days poorer than it looks. Unaffected and working correctly: router benching, the 55s absorption veto, the 5s exhaustion confirm, veto_counter_regime, _BIG_RUN, and data/exit_overrides.json — all key on _base() or on spec.tag consistently. adaptive_exit is off under scale-out by design, not by bug.

2 · Method — and why you can trust the replay

1-minute bars rebuilt from the 5s capture (16,725 bars) using the desk's own agg.MinuteBars folding rule; features computed on the same trailing 60-bar window the live desk holds. Two independent checks that this reproduces the desk rather than approximating it:

checkresult
ATR at entry vs the desk's own recorded entry_atrcorrelation 1.0000, median absolute difference 0.000 pt across 1,632 shadow trades — an exact reproduction, not a fit.
ATR quartiles of the tapeQ25 11.04 / Q50 14.43 / Q75 19.27 pt — independently reproduces Q2's 11.0 / 14.4 / 19.2 from a separate code path.
exit replay vs shadow_real.real_pnl (quote basis)n=558, sign agreement 99.82%, MAE $0.80; $0.00 MAE on 4 of 6 gates. This is a clone of repricer.py, including its two non-obvious rules: entry is the first quote after the signal bar closes (filling at the minute start is lookahead), and fills are at the far touch.
tick basis vs quote basisQuotes only cover 5 days; trade ticks cover 8. On the overlap: sign agreement 99.28%, correlation 0.981, and the tick basis is more pessimistic in every single gate. All headline numbers below are tick-basis (8 days) and cross-checked on the quote basis (5 days).

Populations are the faithful shadow variants whose entry config is byte-identical to the live gate — abs_veto_55s for both abs_veto sides, grind_fast for grind_long — replayed through the live scale-out dual-slot exit (Lot A fixed-R + Lot B per exit_overrides.json, or the built-in default). 644 signals, 8 days, 2026-07-23→07-31. Regime buckets keyed on ATR quartile + 30-min ER + 60-bar range-break, session split at 13:30 UTC.

One population caveat, stated up front: capitulation_long's only faithful proxy is capit_loose, which runs require_flip=False where the live gate runs True. It is a looser superset, so read that gate's rows as directional, not exact.

3 · ★ The test, and the verdict on all five floors

The test is simple and it is the right one. Not trading costs nothing, so a floor only earns its keep if the population it BLOCKS reliably loses money. "Reliably" means three things at once: the blocked book is net negative, it is still negative after you strip its three biggest winners, and it is negative on the worst leave-one-day-out fold. A floor whose blocked book is positive is not a filter — it is a leak.

gatefloorbasisblocked nblocked netstrip best-3LODO worstverdict
