CHOP-DAY VERDICT — so, can we stop donating?

Yes — but with the OFF switch, not with a new gate. The −$249/chop-day donation is recoverable with certainty by benching. The best speculative alternative is +$280/chop-day at t=1.50 selected from 1,152 trials, which the learnability test says will revert to zero. Take the certain money.

The four vetoes this lab genuinely validated are worth more to the router than the gate is, and all four are your own priors, rediscovered independently: (1) don't fade a trending leg (high 60-min ER → −$14.39/trade, t=−3.52); (2) don't fade above ATR ≈30 (−$10.27/trade, t=−2.46) — the exhaustion ATR-gate, rediscovered; (3) don't fade a compressed range (30-min range < 4.2×ATR → −$12.32/trade, t=−3.09) — new, and the strongest single veto found: a compressed extreme is a breakout, not a range test; (4) don't fade the US open (15:06–17:00 UTC → −$8.65/trade, t=−2.07).

If anything gets armed, it is SHADOW-only and clearly labelled unproven: chop_turn_fade — trigger on a new 300s local extreme; vetoes ATR14 ≤ 28, 30-min range ≥ 3.5×ATR, time ≥ 17:00 UTC; fade direction; marketable entry; stop 1.2×ATR, target 1.0R, hold cap 600s; one position at a time, 60s cooldown. Observed n=197 / 6 days, 58.9% win, +$4.42/trade, chop +$280/day, trend +$30. Status: INCUBATE. Needs ~15–20 more captured chop days. Prior expectation given the learnability result: it reverts to ~zero.

Step 1 — the Family A / B split: the census labels the coin after it lands

Before inventing anything I split the 73 census runs by what the label is actually made of. Two families fall out, and they are not equals — one is knowable in advance, the other is a description of what already happened.

FamilyClusterRunsWhat defines the labelKnowable BEFORE the run?
A — flow-labelledFLOW-LED21Heavy 60s aggressor flow (|flow| > 50) aligned with the moveNo — "aligned" is only known once you know the move
VACUUM17Heavy 60s flow pointing the opposite way — trapped aggressorsNo — same coin, other face
B — clockOPEN/NEWS21Run starts inside 13:00–15:00 UTC. Nothing else.Yes — you can read a clock
unlabelledUNCLASS + VOL-EXPANSION14No footprint at all—

Here is the moment the case cracked open. FLOW-LED and VACUUM are the same test with the outcome sign flipped. So I measured the test itself against the tape:

Test on the FLOW-LED / VACUUM labelResult
Base rate of the trigger, all 99,245 bars of tapeP(|60s flow| > 50) = 66.5%
Aligned vs opposed, among runs with |flow| > 50 outside the open window21 aligned vs 17 opposed — binomial p = 0.314
Aligned flow at the 16 FLOW-LED sat-out run starts (z-score)median −0.089, only 44% positive
Volume surge at those same starts (z)0.71 — below the tape median of 0.95
P(a ≥90pt run follows heavy flow) — against flow / with flow / unconditional4.2% / 3.5% / 8.8%
Same, once the tape is hot (ATR15 ≥ 90) — the tilt flips to continuation7.4% against / 13.6% with

Two-thirds of random moments in the week qualify as a "flow event", and given one, the direction is a coin flip that is less likely to produce a run than a randomly chosen moment. Family A is not a cluster. It is a 66.5%-base-rate filter multiplied by a 50/50 coin, named after the outcome. The runs did not start on a flow burst; they started on below-median volume. Everything built to Family A's literal definition is being fitted to noise — and that is exactly what the graves below show, twice over, at two different levels of zoom.

Family B has the opposite property: a clock window is real, ex-ante, and non-negotiable. It is also, not coincidentally, where the only survivor came from.

Step 2 — the oracle: are the runs real money? (yes — so the problem is the ENTRY)

Everything here rests on one premise: that these runs are worth money if you could find their start. The census prices that directly with a hindsight 1-lot ceiling, and this week's honest scoreboard is:

Census outcomeRunsHindsight ceilingWhat a gate actually bankedConversion
CAUGHT14$4,689+$94420%
FOUGHT6$2,286−$248—
SAT OUT53$14,519$0the money on the table

The clean oracle proof came from the survivor itself. NIPC does not know anything about the census — it is a mechanical entry — yet run it over the week and check where it was standing when each run fired:

On-the-run proof (NIPC vs the 21 OPEN/NEWS runs)Value
Runs where NIPC held a position in the run's direction21 / 21
Of those, the exposure finished profitable17 / 21
Net banked on the runs themselves+$1,674
Sat-out subset (the actual gap): aligned / green / net10 / 10 · 6 / 10 · +$189

Entered at the right time on the right side, these runs pay 17 times out of 21. The money is real; the exit is not the problem; the ENTRY is the whole problem.

And now the honest deflation, because I would rather you hear it from me. NIPC converts only $189 of the $3,082 sat-out OPEN/NEWS ceiling — $2,487 of its $2,676 comes from trades that are not census runs at all. It is not a run-capture engine; it is a high-frequency in-window scalper that happens to be present and correctly sided during every big run. Two consequences: do not size this against the $14,519 ceiling — the ceiling is not its addressable market; and the hindsight ceiling in general should be read as a proof the runs exist, not as revenue you can plan around. Even our live gates converted only 20% of the ceiling on the runs they did catch.

Step 3 — ★ THE FINDING: at what SIZE does a footprint become tradeable?

This is the question the full → top25 → top15 narrowing exists to answer, and this week I can finally give you a proper three-part answer instead of a flat "never". The three parts are different kinds of size, and they do not agree with each other — which is itself the useful part.

Kind of "size"Is there a threshold?The measurement
A. Tape-flow footprint size
(how heavy is the aggressor print before the run)		NO — AND INVERTEDcorr(|move|, aligned pre-run flow-z) over the 53 sat-out runs = −0.209. Bigger run → less aligned flow before it. Confirmed by narrowing (table below): the top of the census is emptier of the footprint than the marginal runs.
B. Book / print size
(is absorption a measurable physical quantity)		YES — AND MNQ IS ~6× BELOW ITMNQ: median top-of-book 3.0 lots, top-3 17.2, full visible 10-deep 86.2, against 2,664 contracts/minute of flow = the whole visible book turns over 31× a minute. Median print 1 lot (only 0.62% are ≥10). MES over the same window: top-of-book 23.9, 10-deep 527 — 6.8× / 6.1× deeper.
C. Run size
(given an entry that already works)		YES — ≈195 POINTSNIPC on top-quartile runs (|move| ≥ 195 pt, n=8): 8/8 aligned, 8/8 GREEN, +$1,131 — 68% of its run P&L from 38% of the runs. Marginal runs (|move| < 120 pt, n=5): 3/5 green, +$38, and 2 of its 4 reds live there. Tape-side proxy: impulse span ≥ ~2× ATR1m.

The narrowing evidence for (A), computed this week on the current census:

Run set (sat out)nMedian aligned flow-zMeanFraction > 0
ALL sat-out53+0.116+0.02557%
top25 (the 25 biggest)25+0.038−0.18952%
top15 (the 15 biggest)15+0.100−0.09060%
FLOW-LED sat-out (the cluster named for having the footprint)16−0.089−0.19444%

Hunting harder at the top made it worse, not better. The brief's hypothesis — that narrowing to the biggest runs reveals a stronger footprint the marginal ones wash out — is not merely unsupported, it inverts. And the cluster explicitly defined by having the footprint has the weakest footprint of all four sets.

Why (B) is the most valuable line in the section. Every "absorption" idea the desk has ever entertained — far-side depletion, the wall test, spoof-pull, iceberg refill — is trying to read a signal out of a 3-lot queue that gets replaced 31 times a minute. Our live exhaustion_signal wall test (ask1s ≥ 1.5 × bid1s) is, in practice, 3 lots versus 2 lots. As a sanity check I asked whether MNQ book imbalance predicts anything at all, unconditionally, at 10s / 30s / 60s / 300s horizons across all 6 days: correlation 0.000 to 0.015, non-monotone in every quintile. Zero. That single physical fact independently explains graves G1, G2, G3, G5, G7 in the chop lab and VR-4 in the vacuum lab. The size threshold for a book footprint is roughly an instrument, not a parameter — and MNQ is on the wrong side of it.

The catch on (C), stated plainly: the 195-point threshold is a selection result, not a detection result. It tells you which runs a working entry converts; it does not help you find one. Biasing NIPC toward the big-run footprint does improve the number (RMIN 0.40 → +$2,909; K=1.2 → +$2,603, both on fewer trades) — but both settings sit inside the razor plateau, so that is the same edge concentrated, not a second edge discovered.

GRAVE — FLOWTRAP (the best thing the flow family produced, and it still dies)

The tape-wide scan pointed somewhere genuinely interesting and opposite to the cluster's name: flow aligned with a move predicts ~nothing, while flow opposed to a strong move predicts continuation (−113 points of further downside in the US-session "price collapsing while aggressors keep buying" corner; +37 points in the strongest-prior-move quartile). Trapped counter-aggressors are fuel; confirmed flow is already priced. So I built it: short when price fell hard while they kept buying, vol gate at 15-min range ≥ 70 pt, stop 0.45×range, 2R target, tick-honest exits.

It looked like a winner — +$1,460, PF 1.50, and +$1,632 / PF 1.76 on its home vol segment. It passed leave-one-day-out (every day removable, all remain green: +$881 to +$1,673) and passed the IS/OOS split (+$706 vs +$754). Then three tests buried it:

Kill testResult
Placebo / permutation — circularly shift the flow series, keep everything else identical, 200 draws eachReal +$1,460 vs null mean +$234 (sd $886, 95th pct +$1,723) → p = 0.075; home segment p = 0.145; expvol-US p = 0.105. The real result sits below the null's own 95th percentile in all three. A fake flow filter on the same price trigger already books ~+$600 on this tape.
Strip the best trades+$1,460 → strip-3 +$385 (PF 1.13) → strip-5 −$150. Five trades out of 79 are the entire edge.
Long/short symmetryUS SHORT +$1,434 (n=12) · US LONG −$0.30 (n=9) · ONITE LONG +$346 (n=34) · ONITE SHORT −$184 (n=23). Four sub-populations of n=9–34 with two opposite signs — not one policy, four fitted ones.

It also fails the job it was hired for: 4 of 16 target runs caught, $1,309 of the $14,519 ceiling = 9%. And the lookback window is a step, not a plateau (3min −$236 · 5min −$106 · 10min +$1,487 · 15min +$1,325 · 20min +$815) — two adjacent settings are negative. Buried. One thing genuinely worth keeping: the vol floor is monotone and reusable — range≥0 gives $18/trade, ≥70 gives $31, ≥90 gives $56, while every one of 15 stop×R cells below the floor is red. That re-confirms [[low-vol-grind-untradeable]] mechanism-independently.

Plus the control graves that show why FLOWTRAP looked real: aligned-flow momentum — the census's own literal FLOW-LED read — n=171, +$74, PF 1.01, a dead coin flip (longs −$799 / shorts +$873 cancelling out). Flow-only continuation with no price condition: n=301, −$2,179, PF 0.82. Price-momentum only: n=280, −$889. Contra-flow fade at a 30-min extreme: n=79, −$973. Contra-flow fade with no price condition: +$1,845 — but 07-29 alone is +$1,483 of it, a single-flush artifact.

GRAVE — the VACUUM family: four brand-new signals, four burials, and the participation paradox

Four genuinely new specs, all causal, all two-sided, none reusing an existing gate: VR-1 Vacuum Reclaim (flush → trapped aggressors → reclaim), VR-2 Absorption Fade (heavy flow, no travel, at the extreme), VR-3 Trap Break (delayed confirmed continuation), VR-4 Liquidity Vacuum (the literal L2 reading, using book depth the desk does not consume).

VR-2 was the one worth chasing — a genuine plateau of 54–60% cost-free hit rate across a whole parameter block, not a spike. Its best config nets +$1,301 (n=205, 42.4% win) — then strip-3 takes it to +$596 (and all three best trades are the same clipped max winner), the genuine OOS day pays +$94 ($2.6/trade, neighbour +$11), and across the whole config block only 36% are positive, median −$271, median −$1.45/trade.

The killer table — participation is bought with churn. Across 1,200 configs, corr(runs caught, net) = −0.32:

Runs caught (of 13)ConfigsMedian net% positive
069−$32036%

537−$1,5030%
73−$3,0910%

Cause of death, proven ex-ante. I took the 13 run starts and asked where each candidate feature sat inside 6,000 random live bars. Position in the 2-min range: 53rd percentile, p=1.000 — which kills the "fade at the extreme" premise outright. Absorption (points per contract): 50th percentile — no absorption signature at all. Far-side book depth: 48th percentile — there is no liquidity vacuum in the book. The only "significant" feature is the signed 60-second flow itself, at the 22nd percentile — which is tautological, because VACUUM is defined as opposite flow, and it points the wrong way. The VACUUM label is a post-hoc description of what happened, defined by the sign of the outcome. Nothing measurable at the trigger separates the 13 runs from the ~500 identical-looking setups that go nowhere.

GRAVES — the narrowing levels (prior-week tape), and the survivorship squeeze in the raw

FLOW-LED at top25 and top15 is the cleanest demonstration of the squeeze I have ever produced. At top15 the three target runs carry the cluster's strongest aligned flow (228–421 contracts vs the marginal cluster's 50–155), yet forward that same flow is a 46–53% coin flip at every window and horizon, and strong sell aggression is followed by price drifting up. Then:

ConfigRuns caughtNetOn-run P&LOff-run P&L
FCE W60/TH100 (loose)3 / 3−$5,099n=4 · +$42n=879 · −$5,112
FCE W60/TH250 (base)1 / 3−$3,118n=1 · +$6n=545 · −$3,114
FCE W30/TH750 (least-bad)0 / 3−$681n=0n=86 · −$681

Noise-to-signal 220:1. To fire on all three runs you must fire loose and lose −$5,099; tighten enough to stop bleeding and you catch zero. There is no threshold in between, because forward there is nothing separating the 3 winners from the ~880 look-alikes. All 24 swept cells are red; the loss asymptotes toward zero from below and never crosses positive — and strip-3-best makes it worse (−$681 → −$968), so there is not even a fat tail to point at. The independent breakout variant (RBFC) is red in every cell too. Same story one zoom out at top25: AFC −$5,745 over 851 fires (on-run n=8 +$212, off-run n=843 −$5,956); RBFC −$2,848.

VACUUM at top25 produced the week's most seductive mirage: a trap-reclaim scalp at +$2,110 on 62% win that is pure short beta — SHORT +$2,454 vs LONG +$324, printing only because MNQ fell 1,316 points that week — sitting on a shape knife-edge (reclaim buffer 3 → $3,573, 6 → $2,110, 10 → $718; flow window 45s → −$1,105, 60s → +$2,110, 120s → −$732). Its run-catching sibling fires zero trades on the two biggest runs of that week (+221 and −103). At top15 the best config exists only at n=16, is negative at every tradeable threshold (F100 −$995 through F350 −$202), and strip-3 flips +$242 to −$224 — nine of its sixteen trades are identical −$55 stops.

OPEN/NEWS at the narrowing levels is where the mechanism rotated but the outcome did not: the forward tape said news-window impulses mean-revert (continuation only 35–44%), so a fade looked alive at +$1,534 — until 73% of it turned out to come from 5 survivorship-defined on-target trades, strip-3 removed 58%, and the winning side flipped between candidates (C: long +$656 / short −$249; RIDE: long −$156 / short +$338). At top15 the +$791 fader fights the two biggest runs (−194 and −178, each −$85), catches 2 of 6, sits on a 10-point target knife-edge (t30 −$134 → t40 +$791), and loses −$240 on the one trend day where 3 of the 6 targets live.

The through-line: at every zoom level, in every family, the green numbers are a cooldown scheduling accident, a razor-edge peak, short beta to a down week, or three lucky trades — never a base rate.

Step 5 — THE SURVIVOR: NIPC, the news-impulse pullback (full level, OPEN/NEWS)

Here is where the detective story turns. The prior week buried a Donchian breakout in this exact window, and that grave is what made this week's hunt worth doing properly: it says chasing fails here, which is a fact about the tape, not a verdict on the cluster. So before inventing anything I labelled every 5-second bar in the 13:00–15:00 window across 12 days (17,274 observations) with backward-only features and the forward 15-minute payoff. Two facts reframed the whole problem:

Separability findingConsequence
P(15-min excursion ≥ 90 pt) = 0.499 — half of all bars in this window lead to a census-sized moveOPEN/NEWS is not a detection problem. It is a direction-and-entry-price problem in a tape that is always moving.
A random entry in this window is a −$846 average loser (40-trial Monte-Carlo, same window / stop / exit, sd $472)The window is a meat-grinder. Any candidate must clear a deeply negative null, not zero.
Pre-run aggressor delta is sign-aligned with the run 11 times out of 25Kills the "follow the flow" family and the "fade the flow" family before either is built. (07-31 14:16 ran UP +193 on delta −2,791.)
Position in the 30-min range predicts continuation, not reversionThe direction read of the buried Donchian was right all along. The entry price was wrong.

The mechanical spec — this is the deliverable. Armed only 13:00 ≤ t < 15:00 UTC. Decisions on 5-second bar closes, fills on ticks. Direction is always with the impulse; both sides always armed; no flow or book input at all.

StepRule
1. IMPULSEOver the trailing 2 minutes (24 bars), leg = close[i] − close[i−24]. Require |leg| ≥ 1.0 × ATR1m measured before the impulse (no self-reference). Side = sign(leg). ext = the impulse extreme, org = its origin, span = |ext − org|, require span ≥ 5 pt.
2. PULLBACKWalk forward up to 3 minutes tracking the retrace r = |ext − pullback| / span. If r > 0.75 → ABANDON (structure broken, that was not a pullback). ARM at the first bar where r ≥ 0.35.
3. TRIGGEREntry level = half-back of the pullback (pullback extreme + 0.50 × the retrace distance), and it must still be inside the impulse. Live for 1 minute; fill at the first tick trading through it. No fill → the setup expires, no trade.
4. STOPThe pullback extreme ± a 4-point buffer. R = |entry − stop|. Reject the setup if R < 2 pt. (Median R in practice = 17.2 pt.)
5. EXIT1 lot: fixed 2.5R target. Hard 20-minute time cap; flat by 15:30 UTC. Two-lot desk architecture: Lot A 2.0R / Lot B 2.5R (proven, below).
6. RISKOne position at a time; 2-minute cooldown after exit.
7. REGIMEOFF in dead-chop (ATR1m < 18 pt AND ER15 < 0.35). ON in every other regime.

Why this is a different animal, not a filtered breakout. One ablation settles it — strip the pullback requirement (RMIN=0, RES=0) and NIPC collapses into a plain impulse-chase: −$2,143 at 27.9% win over 290 trades. Same impulse detector, same regime gate, same exit, same tape, same costs. The pullback-and-resume structure is the edge; it is the opposite trade at the opposite price, not a filter bolted onto a breakout.

The backtest, scored per regime on HOME segments only (12 days, 1 lot, 2.5R, $5/RT + a tick of slippage each side). Regime thresholds are taken from this window's own quartiles so every bucket is populated:

The dead-chop gate is not a fitted knob: dead-chop came out negative in all 25 scale-out configs and in every top config of the 270-config exit sweep. It is the one perfectly stable regime finding.

Honest read on the two thin cells: normal-chop LONG (+$67) and violent-whipsaw SHORT (+$35) are flat, not proven — they are "not bleeding", nothing more. But the aggregate split is as symmetric as it gets: long $1,404 vs short $1,272 on a sample of 7 down days vs 5 up days, and −1,055 points of net drift inside the window. This is not a downtrend rider. That single fact is what separates NIPC from every mirage in Step 4.

A real ≥35% retrace actually happening is the edge. Waiting for the resumption instead of catching the falling knife is worth ~$780 on top. The volume filter was monotonically destructive (RV=0 → +$2,474 down to RV=1.4 → +$1,413, costing $976 and 40% of the sample) and was removed, not tuned — inside 13:00–15:00 every bar is already a volume-expansion bar, so RVOL has no cross-section left to cut on.

Your exit cheat-sheet, swept on NIPC entries — proven vs guess. The prior for a momentum gate is Lot A 1.5R / Lot B 2.5R. Two lots, $5/RT each, HOME only:

ClaimProven?
Momentum Lot A 1.5RHOLDS, MILDLY CONSERVATIVE Proven Lot A is 2.0–2.5R, so the guess is ~11% low — but the plateau from 1.5R to 2.5R is broad ($4,642 → $5,352), so the guess sits on the plateau. No rewrite needed.
The standing chandelier / trail prior for Lot BCONTRADICTED IN THIS WINDOW Fixed 2.5R beats a trail decisively ($4,642 vs $3,357 at the same Lot A). Cross-checked on the 1-lot sweep: trail 1.0R@1.0R = +$1,033, worst of 8; fixed 2.5R = +$2,676, best of 8. In a 13:00–15:00 whipsaw the trail is taken out by the counter-leg before the extension resumes.
The tail is real……but only in the trending buckets. Pushing Lot B to 4.0R moves money out of normal-chop (2,108 → 1,303) and into clean-trend (355 → 1,094). A regime-flexed Lot B — 2.5R in chop, 4.0R in clean-trend — is the honest read, but clean-trend n=12–13 is far too thin to bank. Logged, not adopted.
Is the exit surface a plateau or a spike?209 of 270 exit configs (77%) positive; all 25 scale-out configs positive on HOME and all 25 negative in dead-chop. A plateau. No config-hunting was needed.

Where NIPC fails, stated up front. All four red runs (07-28 14:02, 07-29 13:19, 07-31 13:20, 07-31 14:34) are first legs that reversed inside the run window — the impulse was real, the pullback held, and the 15-minute "run" then completed in the other direction after NIPC had been stopped and re-entered. That is the residual failure mode, and the repair is a one-parameter change: a second-entry-in-the-same-direction veto, or an entry-count cap per impulse cluster.

The verdict, and what I want done with it

CandidateLevelHeadlineVerdict
NIPC — news-impulse pullback continuationfull · OPEN/NEWS+$2,676 · n=171 · 12 days · 43.3% · $15.6/trSHADOW — never live yet
chop_turn_fade — chop-day turn scalpchop lab+$4.42/tr · t=1.50 from 1,152 trialsINCUBATE — expect it to revert to zero
Everything in the flow-footprint family (FLOWTRAP, VR-1..4, AFC, FCE, RBFC, trap-reclaim, NWIF)all levels—BURIED

Shadow NIPC, do not arm it. n=171 over 12 days is one regime-fortnight. There is no cross-regime tape here: no FOMC week, no CPI shock, and — critically — barely any genuinely quiet news window (dead-chop is only 34 trades, and that is precisely the bucket the gate turns off). The desk's own promotion ladder says shadow → 1 lot, gated on 8–12 weeks and cross-regime evidence. I am not going to be the one who short-circuits it on a fortnight.

The claim to re-test first is the EXIT, not the entry. "Fixed 2.5R beats a trail in the news window" contradicts our standing chandelier prior and is worth ~$1,285 in the sweep. If that flips on fresh tape, the whole exit half of the spec flips with it.

Fix the census, not the desk. The FLOW-LED / VACUUM rule fires on 66.5% of all bars. It should be a z-score against a trailing 2 hours with a real threshold (|z| ≥ 1), otherwise the two clusters are two names for one coin and next Friday's greenfield agent digs this same empty hole. That is a one-line fix in scripts/run_census.py::cluster() and it is the highest-value change in this section.

The one stone still worth turning: run the chop-scalp harness on MES, 2026-07-12 → 07-17. The L2 lead did not fail because absorption is a bad idea — it failed because MNQ's book is too thin for absorption to be a measurable quantity. On MES the same construct is a real physical measurement (23.9 lots at the top, 527 across 10-deep, 6.8×/6.1× MNQ). The window exists and the blocker is known: alphabot2/data/depth.db holds 10-deep MES book for 07-12→07-31 and alphabot2/data/ticks.db holds MES prints for 07-05→07-17 — they overlap on 07-12→07-17, roughly 4–5 session days with both a tape and a book. Everything is parameterised and re-runnable.

Caveats, stated plainly. One completed week for the census; 12 days for NIPC (6 of them 5-second bar-replay rather than tick-honest — and those are the stricter half, which scores lower, the right sign); 6 days and only 3 chop days for the chop lab; MNQ-only; PAPER. The narrowing-level (top25/top15) hunts are prior-week tape and were not re-run. Every number above is net of at least $5/round-trip with a tick of adverse slippage on entry and exit, stops filling a tick worse and winning ties — deliberately unkind, and it should stay that way.

SURVIVOR: NIPC → SHADOW chop lab: HONEST NULL flow footprint: no size threshold, inverted (−0.209) book footprint: threshold is real — MNQ is 6× under it run size threshold ≈ 195 pt chop days: the OFF switch beats the gate
